checkable.**

## PICS — a two-part AAST consensus guideline, both recovered from full text

**Part 1 (3 Apr 2026, `10.1136/tsaco-2025-002172`):** defines PICS and **ADDS A FOURTH, BIOLOGICAL DOMAIN —
persistent thromboinflammatory dysregulation and sustained organ dysfunction** to the
physical/cognitive/psychological triad. Cognition: **in-person MoCA, TICS as validated remote alternative.**
Physical: **no single test validated across all domains** → individualised, accounting for the surgical cause.
**PICS clinic** = multidisciplinary outpatient programme across four core domains, dedicated provider
champion + nursing + physical therapy, with an explicit feasibility caveat. **Referral: ICU >48 h or high
risk, evaluated before discharge, follow-up 2–4 weeks after.**

**Part 2 (16 Oct 2026, `10.1136/tsaco-2026-002496`):** six questions on patient/family concerns, mental-health
diagnosis, risk factors, what an intensivist can do, caregiver burden, and financial hardship.
**Screening: ICU >48 h or high risk; starts IN the ICU and continues through transitions; HADS for
anxiety/depression, PTSS-14 for trauma/PTSD after discharge "when PTSD-related symptoms can be more reliably
assessed."**
**Risk factors include YOUNGER AGE and FEMALE SEX** (counter-intuitive, stated plainly), and an in-ICU list
that is almost entirely things an intensivist controls → **which is what makes the ABCDEF/ICU Liberation
recommendation substantive: it converts a sedation-and-delirium intervention into a psychiatric one.**

**THE 48-HOUR THRESHOLD is the operational core of both parts, is consistent across them, and is a JUDGEMENT —
nothing in either methods section suggests it came from a risk model.** **Rule: find the single number a unit
would need to implement a guideline, then check whether it was derived or chosen.**

### Published today (brief)

1. Preoperative cognitive training for PND — `10.1016/j.jclinane.2026.112359`, *J Clin Anesth* 116:112359,
   fpd 2026-10-25. **13 RCTs / 1,446 patients. POD RR 0.53 (0.36–0.77), low certainty; DNR RR 0.66
   (0.46–0.96), very low, unsupported by sensitivity analyses. TSA: required information sizes not reached for
   either outcome.** Meta-regression β1 1.072 (0.006–2.138), P = 0.049, traditional over digital training.
   PROSPERO CRD420261293607. **First appearance of cognitive prehabilitation in this archive.**
2. GLP-1 perioperative safety — `10.7759/cureus.116901`, *Cureus* 18:e116901, fpd 2026-09-25. **16 studies:
   7 observational, 9 case reports/series. The denominator asymmetry.** PROSPERO CRD420261289189. Pairs with
   the *Anaesthesia* consensus at `_guidelines/2026-09-20.md`.
3. Classic LMA vs ETT, prone urologic surgery — `10.1155/anrp/1390565`, *Anesthesiol Res Pract* 2026:1390565,
   fpd 2026-10-25. 70 patients, 35/arm, ≤90 min. **Conversion of airway technique is a named primary outcome
   with no result in the abstract.** IRCT20250729066688N1. **First RCT here on prone airway management;**
   topic previously only in `_guidelines/2026-10-04.md`.
4. Perioperative antifibrotic treatment in IPF lung cancer surgery — `10.1111/1759-7714.70408`,
   *Thoracic Cancer* 17, fpd 2026-10-01. **5 retrospective cohorts, pirfenidone. AE-IPF RR 0.24 (30 d) and
   0.23 (90 d); mortality RR 0.12 (30 d) and 0.13 (90 d); I² = 0% for all four.** Searched Ichushi-Web.
   Topic previously in `_guidelines/2026-10-06.md` and `_guidelines/2026-10-09.md`.

### Published today (brief)

1. **Volatile vs intravenous sedation in ventilated critically ill adults** — `10.1177/0310057x261496935`,
   *Anaesth Intensive Care*, 26 Oct. 15 of 3,182 studies. **Delirium RR 0.72 (0.38–1.34, p ≈ 0.31);
   mortality RR 0.97 (0.80–1.17); ICU stay +0.28 d (−1.74 to +2.30); ventilation −37.20 h
   (−78.60 to +4.20), SE 21.12, p ≈ 0.078.** First volatile ICU sedation item in the archive.
2. **Deep sedation protocols for pulsed field ablation of AF** — `10.1111/pace.70457`, *PACE*, 26 Oct.
   166 patients, three electrophysiologist-led protocols (53/67/46). **Target Ramsay ≥ 3: 47.2% / 73.1% /
   82.6% = 25/53, 49/67, 38/46. Dexmedetomidine best sedation and pain, most hypotension (p = 0.047).**
3. **Sedation vs general anaesthesia in paediatric EP studies** — `10.1111/pace.70474`, *PACE*, 26 Oct.
   51 patients, paired within session. **Inducibility 40/51 (78.4%) vs 29/51 (56.9%), p = 0.007, with no
   electrophysiological parameter changed.** Subgroups reconstructed: AVNRT n = 25 (21→15), WPW/AVRT n = 26
   (19→14). **Accessory pathway ERP prolonged under GA, p = 0.018.**
4. **Earlier vs later ECPR in refractory OHCA** — `10.1093/ehjacc/zuag132`, *EHJ Acute Cardiovasc Care*,
   25 Oct. Korean registry 2016–2023, sequential landmark target trial emulation, 22 covariates.
   **909/58,668 = 1.55% treated. ARDs +15.0 / +14.4 / +7.7 points at 15/30/60 min; control survival at
   60 min = 3.2%.** First ECPR timing study here.
5. **Enhanced care unit features and surgical cancellation** — `10.1213/ane.0000000000008309`,
   *Anesth Analg*, 26 Oct. **65 sites, 28 analysable, 4,112 patients. Elective-only beds OR 0.54
   (0.39–0.76); multi-specialty OR 0.48 (0.33–0.69); emergency admissions permitted OR 1.61 (1.06–2.43).**

that does report it.

### Published today (brief)

1. **NasoVISI** — `10.1016/s2352-4642(26)00239-7`, *Lancet Child Adolesc Health*, 27 Oct. 8 centres, 684
   randomised / 673 analysed. **First-attempt success 281/340 vs 249/333; adjusted RD 8.2% (2.0–14.3),
   p = 0.0093. Complications: +0.4% first attempt, −1.9% all attempts, both null. 143 failed first attempts
   (VL 59, DL 84); of 9 patients whose first complication came later, 8 in the DL arm.**
2. **Supraglottic airway vs ETT, sinonasal surgery** — `10.1007/s00405-026-10650-w`, 26 Oct. 9 studies, 714
   patients, **1995–2024 (29 years)**. **One aspiration event. Contamination RR 0.27 (0.15–0.47); day-0 cough
   RR 0.39 (0.23–0.68); sore throat RR 0.65 (0.24–1.74, I² 68.7%). Device contamination "SGAs 0–86%".**
3. **Cochrane, air vs supplemental oxygen at birth** — `10.1002/14651858.cd014781.pub2`, 27 Oct. 12 studies,
   2,054 participants, fixed-effect, all I² 0%. **Mortality pre-discharge RR 0.72 (0.57–0.91, low); HIE II–III
   RR 0.88 (0.71–1.10); mortality post-discharge RR 0.82 (0.58–1.16); NDI RR 1.24 (0.74–2.10, very low). All
   12 studies used 100% oxygen WITHOUT titration. Three of seven outcomes had no data.**
4. **Anticholinergic burden and POD** — `10.1016/j.accpm.2026.101957`, 27 Oct. 10 reports / 9 cohorts /
   10,457 participants. **SMD 0.31 (0.20–0.43); OR 1.22 (1.09–1.37) per point; graded; certainty LOW.**
   PROSPERO CRD420261294941. **Ecological inference flagged.**
5. **OPICU** — `10.1016/j.jclinane.2026.112323`, 27 Oct. **75 ICUs, 55,963 evaluated, 2,764 planned (4.94%).
   804 (29.09%) refused for want of a bed; 411 of the remaining 1,960 (20.97%) did not need ICU. 1,549 =
   78.2% of 1,980, closing exactly. ICU mortality 5.9%, 90-day 7.5%; emergent 18.1% / 18.9%. ORs 12.4 / 3.1 /
   2.7 with no confidence intervals.** **Pairs with the 27 Oct enhanced-care-unit cancellation study.**

   follow-up. Titled "Consensus Guidelines"; methods say "a narrative, evidence-based synthesis".** First

### Published today (brief)

1. **PROPER trial protocol** — `10.1016/j.ahj.2026.107635`, *Am Heart J*, 28 Oct. **PROTOCOL, NO RESULTS.**
   624 patients, 8 Nordic centres, posterior pericardial chest tube vs standard care, primary endpoint AF
   within 7 days, wearable ECG patch, 5-year follow-up. NCT06800781. **Tests a tube where STS (IIa) and APHRS
   (IIa/A) already recommend an incision for the same mechanism.**
2. **DOAC vs VKA for AF after cardiac surgery** — `10.1097/crd.0000000000001516`, *Cardiol Rev*, 29 Oct.
   **5 RCTs, 302 patients (153/149), 26 bleeding events. Any bleeding 9.2% vs 8.1%, OR 1.24 (0.39–3.99), a
   10.2-fold span.** **This is the evidence base beneath the STS/APHRS anticoagulation disagreement flagged
   26 Oct.** **Indexed abstract is TRUNCATED — six named secondary outcomes have no results.**
3. **Paediatric ECPR survival model** — `10.1097/ccm.0000000000007383`, *Crit Care Med*, 29 Oct. ELSO
   2020–2023, derivation 1,060 + validation 317 = 1,377, LASSO, 14 variables. **AUC 0.728 → 0.678; O:E 0.995;
   Brier 0.222; median OR 1.30 (1.11–1.93).** **Abstract never states the survival rate, so the Brier score is
   uninterpretable.** Pairs with the 27 Oct adult ECPR paper.
4. **Desflurane vs sevoflurane and propofol: umbrella review** — `10.1016/j.redare.2026.502163`, 28 Oct.
   **31 systematic reviews, 2014–2024, AMSTAR 2 + GRADE. Faster recovery than sevoflurane at LOW/VERY LOW
   certainty; less consistent vs propofol; no morbidity difference except possible propofol advantage in PONV
   and emergence agitation.** **Pairs with the 28 Oct Singapore sustainability statement.** First desflurane
   item here.

### PREDICTION RECORDED FOR LATER CHECKING

**PROPER's absolute POAF rates will not be comparable with PALACS's**, because PROPER adds wearable ECG patch
monitoring while the guideline trials used routine surveillance. Within-trial comparison is fair; cross-trial
comparison will confound detection intensity with intervention. **Check against the published results.**

### Brief sweep-pattern log — day 10, and the page-size problem is solved

| Step | Query | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| size | `idlist` count, pub window 28–29 Oct | **54** |
| size | `idlist` count, pub window 26–29 Oct | 132 |
| run | `core` pageSize 100, pub window 28–29 Oct | **54 / 54 — PASSED first try** |

**STANDING PRACTICE ADOPTED: count the sweep with `idlist` before running it with `core`, and pick the window
from the count.** Two `idlist` calls are cheaper than one discarded `core` sweep. **Three days of page-size
failures end here.**

### Published today (brief) — five delirium papers, five levers

1. **Remimazolam vs propofol TIVA, POD in neurovascular surgery** — `10.1016/j.bja.2026.09.021`, *BJA*,
   29 Oct. **Double-blind randomised noninferiority, 686 mITT. POD 44/344 (12.79%) vs 37/342 (10.82%); RD
   1.97 (−2.85 to 6.80); margin 10 points = 92.4% of the control rate; upper bound = 62.8% of it.**
   Hypotensive burden median 0 vs 7.5 mm Hg·min; extubation in theatre 85.2% vs 67.3%. NCT06115031.
   **First remimazolam RCT here.**
2. **Dexmedetomidine vs propofol, POD under regional anaesthesia** — `10.1016/j.redare.2026.502211`, 29 Oct.
   **5 RCTs, 1,613 patients; 7.7% vs 16.7%; RR 0.48 (0.36–0.64); I² 13.4%; TSA sufficient; GRADE moderate;
   ARR 9.0 points, NNT 11.**
3. **ARB continuation vs withholding and POD** — `10.1177/00912174261501856`, 29 Oct. **42 analysed, 21/group.
   POD 4/21 vs 12/21; ARD −38.1 (−59.7 to −8.7). BUT orthopaedic cases 12 vs 20 (P = 0.009) — randomisation
   broke on the strongest confounder, and the authors say the result is unattributable.**
4. **Burst suppression duration and ICU delirium** — `10.1007/s10877-026-01508-x`, 29 Oct. **300 patients,
   130 (43.3%) delirious. BSD 81.1 s vs 17.2 s; OR 1.018/s (≈3.1 across the median difference); AUC 0.871;
   cutoff 41.5 s, sens 80.8%, spec 78.2%; BSD+BIS AUC 0.914. PPV 73.9% / NPV 84.2% computed here.**
   **Probably a mediator of sedation depth, and the design supports association, not diagnosis.**
5. **Nurse-led target-directed light sedation in septic ventilated patients** —
   `10.1177/08850666261496192`, 29 Oct. **221 patients by protocol change; delirium 20.4% (20/98) vs 37.4%
   (46/123); adjusted OR 0.428 (0.215–0.854); mortality 28.6% vs 26.0%, P = .671. Before-and-after design.
   DFD-28 named and never reported.**

### STANDING DEFECT TO KEEP COUNTING — a named outcome with no reported result

**Three instances in five days:** 26 Oct prone-airway trial (conversion of airway technique, a *primary*
outcome, no result); 29 Oct DOAC-vs-VKA meta-analysis (six named secondary outcomes, none reported); 30 Oct
nurse-led sedation (DFD-28). **Check every abstract's named outcomes against its reported results, and report
the omission.**

### Symmetry-check exceptions — now two, both recorded

1. **29 Oct:** a median odds ratio's interval (variance-derived statistic, skewed sampling distribution).
2. **30 Oct:** a risk-difference interval at 21 per group (exact or continuity-corrected method).
**Do not flag asymmetry in either case.**

**A document whose subject is what guidelines say or how they have changed belongs to the GUIDELINES WATCH,
whatever journal it appears in.** This morning's brief misrouted the thoracic non-cardiac appraisal to the
literature queue; **the dedupe caught it and the correction is published on today's watch entry.** Remove it
from the brief queue.

### Published today (guidelines)

1. **Thoracic non-cardiac perioperative guideline appraisal** — `10.1097/aco.0000000000001718`,
   *Curr Opin Anaesthesiol*, 29 Oct. **THE DIVERGENCE MECHANISM: guidelines converge on process measures
   (opioid-sparing analgesia, a single chest drain, early ambulation, no reflex ICU admission) and diverge on
   every number (exercise-testing cut-offs, transfusion triggers, drain-removal volumes), with newer documents
   more permissive.** *"Ascertain the underlying guideline generation for any numerical threshold before its
   implementation."* **Limitation: names neither the guidelines reviewed nor how many.**
2. **NANS implantable peripheral nerve stimulation guidelines** — `10.1016/j.neurom.2026.09.002`,
   *Neuromodulation*, 17 Sep. **USPSTF grades — a THIRD grading scheme for the catalogue. Stratified by
   anatomical region then indication; 13 graded indications (4 Grade A, 9 Grade B); ≥ 3 months of failed
   conservative measures as a condition; durability certainty conceded.** **Systematic search closed
   1 July 2025, manual top-up 30 July 2026, published 17 Sep 2026 — 394 and 443 days.**
3. **Loss-of-resistance teaching approach** — `10.52965/001c.172702`, *Orthopedic Reviews*, 26 Oct.
   **Title claims expert consensus; the text calls itself an expert educational perspective by the authors.
   SECOND title/methods mismatch in two days.** Content worth keeping: the transitional stage where the
   supervisor controls the needle and the trainee operates the LOR syringe.
